10 Most Extravagant Video Games of 2026

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Ronald is a video game journalist with more than 10 years of experience in the industry, working on several of the most important Spanish websites.

As someone who's been playing and analyzing games for practically half my life, I've been getting increasingly hard to impress within the interactive entertainment industry after having experienced all sorts of things.

However, this insensitivity to the regular attempts to offer a unique campaign isn't impenetrable, as every year there are developers who manage to break through my shell of apathy and leave a truly strong impression.

Whether they're good or bad games—since the ability to surprise isn't always linked to quality—I always enjoy a campaign that evokes new sensations, given it's become increasingly rare.

Therefore, if you're looking for the kind of adventure that generates feelings you can't even describe, I recommend you try any of these ten most extravagant video games of 2026.

Games that follow in Pokémon's footsteps have left me indifferent for a very long time, but I didn't expect that one of the titles that would hook me the most this year would be Grombo Card Shop Tycoon, the early access version of a game that emulates buying and selling booster packs from the famous franchise.

With simply phenomenal humor and magnificent creature design, brimming with pop culture references, this title strikes a tremendously satisfying balance between incremental mechanics and shop management, becoming exhilarating very quickly.

Dealing with customers looking for specific cards and others who want to bet on whether they'll sell you one for double the price or give it to you for free, deciding what to sell and at what price, decorating to your liking, filling the enormous skill tree... It's a satire that's as conscious and funny as it is addictive.

Never in a million years would I have imagined that opening dozens of booster packs from my own business to find golden feces with a face or a Charizard made of sausage would be so satisfying, but Grombo Card Shop Tycoon made it a reality.

Remember the LCD aesthetic of the Game & Watch? Me neither, until I stumbled across Future Knight by accident and fell completely in love with its take on a rather forgotten variant of retro gaming.

Based solely on its glorious and unique visual style, the title's presence on this list is practically essential, but if we add the plot, a homunculus tumor inside a robot who travels to the past to defeat the tyranny of a cultist who erased humanity's memory and set up a pyramid scheme as a cult, it becomes even more compelling.

Regardless, the gameplay is equally impressive, as you control both the android, based on a 90s soap opera protagonist, and the tiny companion, resulting in an extremely fun 2D action-platformer with challenging bosses and spectacular animations.

Given its arcade-style focus, Future Knight lasts three hours yet will win you over for life, especially since, unless you're approaching old age, I'm sure you've never played anything remotely similar.

Ever since I played Ghostrunner at launch, I've been forever linked to One More Level and everything bearing its name, though even with that affection, I couldn't have imagined what Valor Mortis would bring.

The Soulslike genre is usually quite traditional and rigidly adheres to its conventions, but the studio decided to focus more on its own expertise than on FromSoftware's legacy, resulting in the only game of its kind where movement is a delight.

William inherits Jack's motion abilities in a first-person action title that's an ARPG, an FPS, and a parkour experience simultaneously, blending all sorts of gameplay concepts and philosophies that one wouldn't think should work together, yet they do so magnificently.

Add to that magnificent worldbuilding in an apocalyptic France of Napoleon Bonaparte, some of the best level design the genre has ever seen, and particularly high production values compared to its peers, and Valor Mortis becomes a rather particular specimen.

If you want to spend the autumn in the safest yet most violent way, I invite you to play the ten best action games that you need to play in fall 2026.

Faith: The Unholy Trinity is among the best horror video games I've ever played, and Tenebris Somnia became the finest spiritual successor I could have imagined for such an outstanding production.

Instead of rotoscoping, the game uses cinematic cutscenes with live actors and real sets, complemented by spectacular makeup and editing work that blends seamlessly with the title's refined pixel art to create otherworldly, bizarre, and terrifying creatures.

From its themes and levels to its creative puzzles and fascinating story and mythology, Tenebris Somnia is one of the most distinctive references the genre has seen in the entire decade, as easy to spot among hundreds of titles as finding an anvil in a haystack.

Because of its fundamental ideas and the perfect pacing with which it moves between video game and cinematic experience, you'll never expect the kind of story that awaits you, much less the fidelity with which that story is translated into gameplay.

Inside the AAA spectrum, few developers are as functionally eccentric as Capcom, which, as Pragmata demonstrates, doesn't need to sacrifice experimentation to deliver a truly enjoyable title.

The campaign unfolds through the dichotomies of hacking and shooting, Hugh's brutishness and Diana's innocence, the slow pace and dynamic levels, and so on with every element that constitutes it, because it doesn't run away from the tension of conflicting concepts.

It's an emotional and charismatic story, set against its backdrop so that, along with the spirited gameplay, it never stops moving forward, always feeling like a fresh and captivating experience that you never want to stop playing.

Most big-budget products avoid contradictions and risks, and therein lies Pragmata's extravagance: going against the grain of its class and prioritizing its creative vision above all else.

Kyle Thompson's craftsmanship is becoming increasingly refined with each new Metroidvania he releases, but he has truly outdone himself in every conceivable way with Well Dweller.

Speaking of contrasts, his latest adventure leaves you feeling both profoundly unsettled and captivated by its environments and inhabitants, as the juxtaposition of gorgeous biomes and atmospheric music with gothic and unsettling designs is mesmerizing.

Fighting bosses whose behaviors and aesthetics seem lifted from sleep paralysis is magnificent, even when it involves giant hands chasing you through the pipes of a kingdom, a dollmaker trapping your sister's soul in a plush toy, or a guardian decapitated by the moon to possess his body.

Well Dweller is a collection of fables and legends seen through the lens of its action-adventure gameplay, which, needless to say, is extraordinary and offers some of the best structures in the entire genre in terms of combat, movement, exploration, and progression.

The strategy genre displays extraordinary depth, and offers some truly unique and underrated titles.

"Extravagant" means unusual, striking, and peculiar, so I can't think of better adjectives to describe Phantom Blade Zero, which continues Black Myth: Wukong's work in breaking down cultural barriers related to China.

The video game industry of this Asian giant had only previously showcased its talent on a large scale in the mobile ecosystem, but projects like S-Game's open our minds to the world of possibilities that comes with seeing the interactive medium through their eyes.

The result? A glorious action game that can't be reduced to being inspired by one or another benchmark of the genre, but rather driven by Chinese idiosyncrasies in terms of functionality, visual identity, narrative, mechanics, and sound, offering a unique journey both on paper and in execution.

Among everything released this year, Phantom Blade Zero is by far one of the most exciting and culturally significant products, something we can't say about half of the creations that boast its production values.

If Remedy Entertainment ever releases a video game that isn't an enlightening, mind-blowing experience, I'll be the first to speak out, but Control Resonant shows us that we'll still have to wait to see if that day ever comes.

The sheer audacity of making such drastic changes from its predecessor, especially after Alan Wake 2's financial hardships, is the kind of gallantry we must praise, support, and cherish to keep the art of video games alive, because it transcends mere commercial success.

Consequently, we got a title whose story, setting, characters, enemies, and even gameplay systems transcend the ordinary, becoming part of those special titles you can only sum up as "I can't tell you about it; you have to experience it".

Even though Control Resonant isn't among my top titles from the studio, it's still magnificent and light-years ahead of what the average developer can achieve, proving once again that Remedy simply operates in a league of its own.

If you’re looking for fast-paced titles to violently lose yourself in, here are ten action games most people haven’t played but definitely should.

The name Edmund McMillen has become synonymous with irreverence, weirdness, and iconic status, as you can see games like Mewgenics from miles away and still have every fiber of your being know who created it and what to expect.

However, even with full awareness of its creator's unsettling talent, mere mortals aren't prepared to anticipate the absurd display of depth, inventiveness, and ability to cohere oddities into one of the most addictive products in video game history.

I don't know what it says about our industry that such adjectives can be applied to a work that consists of breeding cats with autism, ADHD, cancer, bipolar disorder, and other conditions to battle in turn-based duels against "fetus-like enemies", feces, or angel pigeons, but I'm glad nonetheless.

If you have the willpower to dedicate less than hundreds of hours to Mewgenics, I applaud you, but I'm not that strong, because I'm afraid I have a weakness for roguelites that have more quirkiness than pixels.

Now, the 2026 game that most often left me speechless, simply because I couldn't believe what I was seeing, is Romeo is a Dead Man, which is completely to be expected if you know who Suda51 is.

Author-driven video games aren't as common a phenomenon as they are in film, but Goichi Suda is an exception to the rule precisely because of how he can take a story so often repeated throughout the centuries across every imaginable artistic medium and still make it original.

You think you know the Romeo and Juliet fable, though Romeo is a Dead Man thoroughly subverts it to create a title with mechanics from hack and slash, visual novel, on-rails shooter, third-person shooter, creature management, and other genres I can't even name.

An intergalactic, interdimensional, and intertemporal twisted love story that plays with the rules of the universe as much as with our expectations couldn't help but be extravagant, yet that's just how the great Suda51 works.

I'd venture to say it's far from being the best game on the list, but that hardly matters because, when it comes to being unique, everyone would have to ask for its blessing just to even enter the conversation.

From the distant ICO to the recent Desktop Explorer, these are ten adventure games you can finish in under 10 hours.

10 Sega Genesis JRPGs Most Haven’t Played, But Definitely Should

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Zackari Greif is a List Writer at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2021. A lifelong gamer and former writer for GameRant and Fix Gaming Channel, Zackari has written across news, guides, interviews, previews, reviews, features, and lists, bringing a broad background in gaming journalism to h

When you think of the JRPG subgenre, you think of PlayStation or the SNES. I can’t refute that Sony and Nintendo earned their stake in that video game niche as a big Kingdom Hearts and Final Fantasy 7 fan. But there’s one other console that had an expansive library of titles in that area.

Even though it was most known for its war against Nintendo and the start of Sonic the Hedgehog’s 35-year-long running streak, the Sega Genesis had a number of good JRPGs on it over the years. Games like Phantasy Star and Landstalker have been widely noticed, but most of them are only recognized by die-hard Sega fans.

Sega's 16-bit console era has left its mark on the video game industry in ways we still see today.

As a big Sega fan myself, (to a point,) I’m here to help navigate you through some Sega JRPGs you’ve most likely left behind or just weren’t around to experience. Don’t worry, we’ve got plenty to dig into.

Back in the day, and even now, Crusader of Centy has been seen as Sega’s The Legend of Zelda clone. In truth, it has a few elements that help separate it from Link’s adventures, but the biggest is that it was actually made as part of a big Genesis-based RPG push in Japan. We’ll be talking about more of these games as we go.

Out of all the games released through this initiative, I feel like Crusader of Centy is the most well-known. But out of the people who know about it, there’s a stark divide. Retro JRPG fans have likely played it, but how many Sega fans outside of that have actually experienced it? The only thing you might know about it is the fact that Sonic’s in it. Yeah. I said it.

I believe it’s a game really worth playing. It harkens back to an era where RPG and adventure game mechanics weren’t so strictly divided. Crusader of Centy also focuses pretty heavily on exploration and discovery, namely in the way that animal companions give you abilities you only get through mixing them up. There’s a lot here for almost everyone.

I know, I know, calling Beyond Oasis a JRPG is a little controversial. Its top-down, open combat and varying challenges make it hard to call it much of anything. That’s the trouble with these games sometimes. Genres were hardly the big defining points of a game as they are now, and a lot of games got to experiment with what they offered.

In the case of Beyond Oasis, if it has story beats like an RPG, has magic you can level up like an RPG no matter how basic, and has rewards for exploring through the map like an RPG, then it’s an RPG. Even if you disagree, I’m sure you agree that more people need to play this game. It’s one of the few Sega Genesis games that play this way, and it’s gorgeous to look at on top of it. If you haven’t played Beyond Oasis, I highly recommend it.

You’ve likely heard of the Shining Force series if you’re familiar with retro JRPGs. It’s one of the most highly regarded options for the genre that the Sega Genesis had, coming second to Phantasy Star itself. The first two games are on Nintendo Switch Online’s Classic offerings, even. But because of a number of different factors, like its name or its formula changes, Shining in the Darkness isn’t mentioned a lot because it hasn’t been played a lot.

For those not in the know, many of the Shining Force games are tactical RPGs. Shining in the Darkness is the first game in the series, and it was a dungeon crawler instead. It’s a very classic dungeon crawler, too, resembling a strange combination between Dragon Quest’s presentation and Wizardry’s systems.

It very much feels like a Shining game through and through, thankfully. It’s spectacularly paced like the later games are, which makes it a great introduction to the genre. It’s also less of a pain than some of it’s contemporaries at the time. We’ve all had that struggle where a retro game is a bit tougher than we expected, right? You won’t run into that here.

For every Fire Emblem and Final Fantasy Tactics that have managed to beat the odds and become popular and prominent tactical JRPGs, there’s about ten other games that didn’t get as big of a chance to shine. To me, Warrior of Rome is one of them. It leans a bit more close to something like Civilization but it I feel like I’m just playing a Fire Emblem competitor above anything else.

And it’s very fun! The first time I discovered this game, I kind of sat there and played it for three hours, completely transfixed. It’s a fairly rudimentary game, but ordering my soldiers and watching them all fight was still entertaining, even if the graphics weren’t the best the Genesis could do. Besides, Warrior of Rome tracked multiple battles I had across the map in real-time, and that was really impressive to see on the Genesis of all things. I feel like more tactical JRPG fans could try this game out. It’s a nice little snack while you wait for the bigger pillars in this little niche.

We’re back at another Zelda-like action RPG of the old days! Togi O: King Colossus is one of those games that just gets you right into what you want. You’re given simple exposition, you get a mission from the old man you live with, and get out there. If only the game was that easy on its players.

Togi O is a game with a lot of fighting. For some of you, that’s great. For others, it’ll be the most annoying thing you’ve ever dealt with on the Sega Genesis. Enemies are allowed to deal whatever punches they can throw at you, while nine times out of ten, you’re going to be left with a low-range weapon to use. It’s not exactly fair.

You also need to defeat all these enemies In crowded dungeon rooms to get items you need to progress. Items that are part of one big fetch quest, mind you. Argh. But even with all of this, Togi O might be the last stone left unturned for some of you die-hard fans out there. And like Crusader of Centy, it’s a good, retro relic that could use more eyes on it. Give it a try if you want a bit of a nostalgic challenge.

There are many elements and mechanics that JRPGs borrowed from other genres to create their wonderful works.

Back in 1991, Sega entered a strange, fleeting partnership with what’s one of the more obscure yet important JRPG developers out there, Nihon Falcom. This partnership would only last around four years, but it would lead to some important ports of Falcom games under and joint name “Sega Falcom.” One of these would be Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes.

Now, Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes was on a number of consoles, including the SNES, but the Genesis version in particular has impressively better visuals. Plus, if you’re here, you’ll likely prefer the Genesis sound font anyway.

Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes on Genesis does away with the big, bordered display with all of the menus around it and gives you the entire screen to work with. This allows sprites to breathe and big moments and battles to shine more because of it. Even if you’ve played Dragon Slayer: The Legend of Heroes some other way, like maybe with its PSP portable remake, I still think this version is worth experiencing. It really shines on the Genesis. And that might be through the partnership that helped it thrive.

Now this is where things get interesting. There are a lot of great JRPGs on the Sega Genesis that everyone has likely played, but even at the bottom of the barrel, there’s some hidden gems. Braver Battle Saga: Legend of the Magic Warrior is one of those games. It’s an unlicensed Sega Genesis game that dates back as far as 1996. It’s earned notoriety for being a pretty good game despite lacking the golden Sega seal of approval on the box.

It’s a classic JRPG through and through despite that. Complete with quaint little towns, a rip-off ATB system, and battle music that will make you hate how many encounters there are in this game. Great for players who think the JRPGs of the olden days are better than what we’re getting now. Because of how great this game feels and plays, it’s been restored officially with modern releases and an English translation that you can find on Steam. If you’ve been craving a retro hidden gem to come out of nowhere like this, then this game is exactly what you’re looking for.

If you loved Final Fantasy Resonance, then it's time for more JRPG love, Jobs, and pixel art.

If you’ve been digging through Sega Genesis games, looking for all the RPGs that the system has to offer, then you might have heard of this one before. I tend to harp on this one a lot because it’s a baffling and head-spinning obscure game that I feel like those self-proclaimed experts of the genre should check out. Maten no Soumetsu is well-known for being impressive visually and incredibly punishing with its difficulty.

If you feel incredibly powerful because you beat Elden Ring, or just want to suffer with a game that’s truly unforgivingly hard, I dare you to pick up this one and report back to me. Good luck!

If you’re here, I’m assuming you really like mechas. Or anime. Or Fire Emblem. Happen to be a fan the first and third ones? Boy, do I have the game for you! Vixen 357 is a war strategy JRPG that brings mechas and the same staples of Fire Emblem to one fantastic Sega Genesis game.

As you might expect, you control units in the form of human-piloted mechs. I state this kind of obvious fact because Vixen 357 pulls no punches, and it shows you the pilots bleeding before the permadeath mechanics take them away. This, combined with some gorgeous graphics, is why Vixen 357 has been saved and has recently been ported to modern consoles through Ratalaika Games and Shinyuden. Go check it out!

Remember when I spoke about an initiative back in the day that aimed to put more JRPGs on the Sega Genesis in Japan? Some games made its way over, but not all of them. Surging Aura is one of those games. It’s circled around the import and translation scene for years as a great game that was locked behind the lack of a full translation.

I’m happy to report that in the time since I last wrote about Surging Aura, a full English translation patch has been completed! Yes, you’re reading this right. There’s a brand-new (sort of) JRPG that you’ve likely never played ripe for the taking right now! Surging Aura feels like it was meant to be Sega’s answer to Final Fantasy, but it has its own distinct personality and charm to where it feels like its own unique adventure.

We have the character and monster design of the late Mutsumi Inomata to thank for that. If you’re big on JRPGs, that name might sound familiar. She was the character designer for the Tales of series starting with Tales of Destiny. So, if you’ve been a fan of her work, this game is calling out to you specifically. It’s worth your time, I promise.

While some JRPGs disappear, others return from oblivion after years without new releases.

Indie App Spotlight: ‘CueFlip’ automatically turns the page for your sheet music

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Welcome to Indie App Spotlight. This is a weekly 9to5Mac series where we showcase the latest apps in the indie app world. If you’re a developer and would like your app featured, get in contact.

If you find yourself playing an instrument with sheet music, flipping the page often requires you to stop playing to turn the page. CueFlip solves that. You can teach it how you play a song, then it can turn the page for you whilst listening.

CueFlip doesn’t just assume how it should turn the page, you can explicitly teach it how to. That starts with either a live rehearsal, or a recording you can share with it. Then, you mark the turns.

CueFlip also has a built in dictionary, where you can see certain notes on a page so you can learn how the composer meant to play them, rather than needing to guess them. Many times, these terms will be in other languages, but CueFlip offers built in translation with up to 13 languages. CueFlip covers over 20,000 terms, and it can pull terms from scanned scores as well.

Lastly, CueFlip has a neat little feature with Face ID. If you wink with your right eye, you can manually turn a page forward, and if you wink with your left eye, it’ll go backwards. Similarly, CueFlip also works with manual Bluetooth page turners in addition to the automatic turning, in case you need it.

Here’s why 15 year old classical guitarist Yuze Pan from Beijing decided to build CueFlip:

I play in the Primal Guitar Quartet, and page turns kept breaking up our rehearsals. Paper means taking a hand off the strings, a pedal or a wink is one more thing to remember in the middle of a phrase, and timed turns can’t follow a performer who speeds up and slows down. My first attempt, in 2025, didn’t work. In May 2026 I started again, this time by reading the research on audio alignment, and brought every new build to the quartet’s daily rehearsals.

CueFlip is available for free on the App Store for iPhone and iPad users running iOS and iPadOS 16.4 and later. The free version includes PDFs with annotations and automatic page turning for one score, whereas Pro unlocks unlimited page turning, the music dictionary, wink to turn pages, and much more for $4.99/month and $29.99/year.

How long should a PC processor actually last?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

If you treat it right, it should last until it's obsolete.

Building a new PC is a major investment, especially with prices of some components skyrocketing recently. Given the expense, it's natural to wonder not just if you can afford it now, but how long a PC should last if you do fork over the cash to build one. The central processing unit (CPU) is one of the biggest expenses in building a PC, but you likely won't need to buy a new one too often.

Under most conditions, you don't need to worry about your processor wearing out from regular use. Manufacturers don't list specific expected lifespans for their processors, but it's not uncommon for a CPU to remain functional for a decade or more after it's installed. Rather than your CPU failing, it's much more likely that you'll eventually want to replace it when it can't keep up with the new games or programs you use your PC for.

The most common time for a processor to fail is when it's still new. The manufacturing process can produce faulty CPUs from time to time, and when that happens, they'll likely give you trouble from the start. If you've had your CPU for a while, some of the signs that it's failing are your computer freezing for no clear reason, failing to boot or randomly restarting. All of these problems can be caused by other hardware, though, and pinning the problem to the CPU is as difficult as it is rare.

If your computer isn't performing as you'd like it to, it's worth checking if your CPU is just outdated. First, pull up your PC's Task Manager and check the Performance tab. Keep an eye on the CPU screen to see how much of the processor's power is being used. Under normal conditions, it should almost always stay below 20 percent. Some spikes are normal, but if it's constantly approaching its upper limits, you might want to consider an upgrade. Your CPU will be much more active during intensive tasks like gaming, but if it stays at 90 percent or more, especially if your GPU usage stays low at the same time, that could be cause for concern.

The biggest stressor to your PC is heat, which degrades performance of every component over time. The best thing you can do for your PC's longevity is to make sure it has adequate airflow by properly installing fans and regularly cleaning dust out of the case. Programs like HWiNFO let you monitor your system's temperature — if your CPU rises above 60 degrees Celsius under normal load or above 80 degrees during intensive tasks, it's worth looking into what improvements you can make. These numbers can vary based on the particular CPU, though, so it's best to follow manufacturer recommendations if they're available. If temperatures are high, you should also consider replacing the thermal paste on your CPU, which can last five years or more but eventually becomes less effective at regulating heat.

Overclocking your CPU — increasing its speed beyond the manufacturer's recomendations — can also reduce its lifespan, though usually it will still last until it's obsolete if you've overclocked safely. Other than time and heat, the most likely threat to your CPU is damage from a power surge or a faulty power supply. While rare, these are still more likely than a CPU simply "going bad" with age. It can be difficult to pinpoint exactly which component is failing without going to a professional technician, but with even minimal care, your CPU should keep ticking well past the point when it's time for an upgrade.

Mathys Tel, the big controversy!

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Tough sequence for Mathys Tel. Friday, the Tottenham striker could hope to appear in Zinédine Zidane’s last list for the France A team. With the injuries (Dembélé, Lepaul, Akliouche, etc.), there was a small opening, a place to take, finally seized by Pablo Pagis (Paris FC).

The Rossoblu had got off to a truly disastrous start under former Belgium international coach Tedesco, scraping one point from the opening four Serie A matches.

All three defeats were 1-0 scorelines, and he was fired on September 16 to bring in Palladino.

It has been a complete transformation, drawing the opener and then winning five of the next six games, faltering only 3-1 to Inter.

“I don’t know how far this team can go, but we want to enjoy ourselves together. We rediscovered our smile.”

Trump targets Slotkin in call for DOJ probe of alleged Democratic threats to Republicans

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump said Saturday that he was ordering the Justice Department to investigate Democratic leaders over what he described as threats against Republican officials, donors and supporters, without identifying the alleged communications or providing evidence of criminal conduct.

"The Democrats are going around threatening a lot of people, including the Attorney General of the United States, and others, with Impeachment and many other forms of punishment, even though they admit they have no reason to do so," Trump said in a Truth Social post.

The alleged threats, the president said, are "extortion at the highest level," he wrote. "I am ordering the Department of Justice to look into this criminal act, immediately!"

In his post, Trump claimed that Democratic Sen. Elissa Slotkin of Michigan is planning to "go after all young people that believe in Republican Values."

Trump was apparently referencing a speech Slotkin made at a University of Chicago event on Oct. 13. At the time, Slotkin said: "I want young people coming out of school to say, 'Should I go work for the Trump administration?'...I want them to be like, 'Lord, that might be buying myself a ton of problems that I don't want. And so let me avoid participating in that.' I want that deterrence message sent."

Slotkin alleges that she, her family and her staff faced physical and legal intimidation after the posting of a November 2025 video in which Slotkin and other Democratic leaders called on the U.S. military to "refuse illegal orders" following the airstrikes targeting boats in the Caribbean.

On Nov. 20, Trump called the video treason and said "an example must be set."

The FBI contacted lawmakers, including Slotkin, days later. In February, Slotkin declined to sit for an interview the Justice Department requested about the video.

"The intimidation is the point, and I'm not gonna go along with that," the senator said in February. A federal grand jury later declined to indict the video participants.

Now, Trump alleges Democrats are threatening legal intimidation against Republican leaders and their supporters.

"Republicans should get smart, fast! Threatening letters have been sent to many people, all innocent, from Democrat Political Leaders," the president wrote.

The White House and Slotkin didn't immediately respond to a request for comment.

Bill Skarsgard Says He’s “Spent” Playing Bad Guys but Would Still Play a James Bond Villain

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The 'Nosferatu' star also said he wasn't approached to play the next 007: "I wasn't even thrown into the mix."

Bill Skarsgard is feeling “spent” when it comes to playing villains.

The 36-year-old actor is known for taking on dark characters, including Pennywise in the It movies, Count Orlok in Nosferatu, Eric Draven in The Crow and Marquis Vincent Bisset de Gramont in John Wick: Chapter 4. But during a recent episode of Josh Horowitz’s Happy Sad Confused podcast, Skarsgard explained that he’s becoming more selective about taking on villain roles.

“For a villain, it would have to be something that felt original to me, that was something that I hadn’t done before,” Skarsgard said. “I feel, kind of, spent with those things. But there’s villains [that] could be really fun in different ways and if it’s the right kind.” Related Stories Movies Josh O'Connor Calls Bond Speculation "Incredibly Bizarre Pinch Me Moment" Movies Pierce Brosnan Supports Callum Turner Being Cast as James Bond: "So Strong..."

Horowitz then brought up Skarsgard’s longtime interest in playing a villain in the James Bond franchise.

“Yeah! Well, because they didn’t call me about James Bond. I don’t know what that was about,” Skarsgard quipped. “Maybe they were like, ‘We can’t do a Swede for this guy.’ I wasn’t even thrown into the mix.”

The Dead Man’s Wire actor agreed with Horowitz that he wouldn’t necessarily want to commit to leading the James Bond franchise for 10 or 15 years. Instead, he said he’d be interested in taking on a one-off villain role, citing Mads Mikkelsen and Javier Bardem as examples.

Skarsgard’s comments come as the search for the next actor to play 007 continues ahead of Denis Villeneuve’s upcoming film. The Hollywood Reporter previously reported that Callum Turner, Jack Lowden and Josh O’Connor had screen tested for the part. However, on Friday, THR reported that Robert Pattinson had also screen tested, while Nicholas Hoult’s name has emerged as another potential contender.

As for Skarsgard, he stars in Peter Berg’s war film The Mosquito Bowl, which is currently playing in theaters and set to release on Netflix Nov. 25.

Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 Goes Free for a Limited Time

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

Call of Duty will always be a major force in the gaming zeitgeist; that much is certain, but the recent success of Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 was a much-needed win for the franchise. With the campaign receiving fantastic reception, the multiplayer rolling out, and the inclusion of the extraction mode DMZ, Activision is looking to keep racking up wins.

Former Skyrim lead designer Bruce Nesmith says Todd Howard knows when to make things simpler for players, and recalls a design decision that proved him wrong.

Their newest incentive was announced on the official Call of Duty Twitter: Modern Warfare 4’s DMZ is free to play for all until November 11th, giving new and returning players a taste of extraction action exclusive to the Modern Warfare reboot series. Serving as MW’s answer to Zombies, the mode has been totally revamped in preparation for new blood.

DMZ has stuck with the Modern Warfare reboot series since MW2, serving as the title’s unique mode alongside Black Ops Zombies. While Zombies leaned into the absurd paranoia and conspiracies that swirl around undercover operations, DMZ focuses on the realism and boots-on-the-ground feel that originally made Warzone work so well, playing off Modern Warfare’s thematic strengths.

MW4’s DMZ dropped only a few days ago, but has already hit the ground running, with story missions, PvE encounters, and plenty of incentives to play cool or callous when other players show. Packed with new weapons, gear, and locations, MW4’s DMZ is set on the Korean Peninsula, Hajin, where the devastation that rocked the campaign is still felt.

Stuck between squadrons of NPC soldiers, a dangerous map, and the X factor of players, the free trial will populate Hajin even more, sparking hijinks and carefully curated tactics in equal measure. Any progress made by players during their free playtime will translate to progression in the main game, giving prospective players a hit of the game’s action without asking for the full $69.99.

With the campaign receiving fantastic reception, the multiplayer rolling out, and the inclusion of the extraction mode DMZ, Activision is looking to keep racking up wins.

That price tag is honestly the biggest pain point for many players, but Modern Warfare 4’s new rewards for completing weapon progression, multiple modes with a variety of unlocks, and a phenomenal upgrade of the franchise’s gunplay make it a solid buy for anyone content with sticking to a single title for a while.

MW4’s reinventions were fully needed to pull the subseries out of the total rut that was Modern Warfare 3, and it’s incredibly exciting to see where the game will go both with its internal modes and as a guiding star for the franchise going forward. Black Ops might be up next on the rotation, but there’s no shame in taking notes from your neighbor.

Call of Duty stands alongside Grand Theft Auto as major gaming icons that have avoided aligning with any specific platform. While GTA 6 is releasing on PC at a later date, Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 4 dropped everywhere simultaneously, even on the Switch 2. The title’s sales give universal releases a good argument, even as console first-party titles are being tied back down.

With the gaming community finally back on its side, Modern Warfare 4 has some big opportunities to recover the franchise’s PR, which has been stumbling after questionable cosmetics and stuttering quality. Modern Warfare 4 is sticking to its aesthetic and goals well for now, but time will only tell if Warzone’s inclusion will make odd collabs start slinking in.

Infinity Ward has some time to rest on their laurels and focus on live service updates, with Season 1’s launch already bringing players back in. With a full DMZ trial, a good chunk of players are sure to stick around, even if those already in the game treat them like easy pickings for the duration.

As GTA 6's launch approaches, everyone wants part of the PR pie, including internet ISPs.

Hotel Refills Toiletries From Ketchup-Style Bottles—This Is Why You Can’t Trust What’s In The Shower

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

This upscale hotel’s housekeeping cart looks ready to dress a hamburger: red, yellow and white squeeze bottles lined up beside the guest supplies. The red one says “Body Wash.” In the shower, the bottles say Klur. Travel journalist Scott Mayerowitz photographed the setup and compared it to the condiments at McDonald’s. He’s glad the hotel is refilling its toiletries. I’m looking at the same photo and wondering how guests are supposed to know what they’re actually washing with. A post shared by Scott Mayerowitz (@globetrotscott) His follow-up photo on LinkedIn shows Klur Repose shampoo, conditioner and body wash in the shower, in the Clary Sage + Lavender scent. My guess, eyeballing the hallway and bathroom, is the Omni Boston Hotel at the Seaport. Readers familiar with the property can confirm or correct that. A September review documents Klur toiletries there, and Klur supplies Omni more broadly. The red squeeze bottle isn’t literally unlabeled. It’s self-labeled. The bottle identifies as body wash. What it doesn’t show is whose body wash. The contents may be genuine Klur, though it seems odd if it were that it would be in a ketchup squeeze bottle. Best case, this looks cheap. They went to the trouble of selecting a branded amenity for the bathroom, then made the refill process look like the back of a burger joint. I’ve Reported This At Hyatt Hotels, Too In May 2025, I wrote about Hyatt Regency San Francisco’s ketchup-style refill jug. A reader photographed an unbranded squeeze container being carried with housekeeping supplies. Guests could see the dispenser in their bathroom, but couldn’t identify the product supplying it. Then, in December, a reader found condiment containers on a housekeeping cart at Hyatt Place Phoenix Mesa. That cart also had a toilet brush beside fresh towels, which didn’t improve the presentation. At Hyatt’s Wild Palms Hotel in Sunnyvale, California, a reader photographed DRIFT bulk toiletries on housekeeping carts while the guest bathroom bottles displayed Jonathan Adler branding. The hotel advertised Jonathan Adler amenities. When One Mile at a Time followed up in January, Ben reported that the labels had been removed from the bulk jugs, and published new photographs. The hotel was still advertising Jonathan Adler. And at the Suining Marriott in China, management there actually acknowledged swapping out cheaper stuff. A guest found FORMETURE products instead of Marriott’s thisworks amenities. The general manager said the owner had changed products to save money. The hotel denied that the replacement was counterfeit, saying it was a legitimate product with quality documentation. It promised to restore the brand standard, and the guest received 8,000 points. Does This Break Any Rules? Klur’s hospitality products are is sold in bulk refill containers. I’d expect those to be used rather than squeeze bottles. Putting a different product into a Klur-labeled bottle could create a misbranding problem. Handling cosmetics under conditions that could contaminate them can violate adulteration rules. And passing off a cheaper product as the promised brand could also run afoul of Massachusetts law against deceptive business practices. Bulk Toiletries Leave More For The Guest To Worry About I’ve been criticizing this move for years. Buying larger containers can reduce packaging (but they can opt for single use biodegradable packaging too). It also lets hotels spend less on toiletries. I’ve shown a Sheraton’s bulk toiletries sitting beside disposable plastic cups, with plastic water bottles elsewhere in the room. They were surely using bulk toiletries out of an ideological commitment to end plastic use. The refill routine adds opportunities to mix up products or introduce contamination. TA 2023 study of 104 hotel soap dispensers in Germany found bacterial contamination in 70.2% of refillable pump dispensers, compared with 10.6% of nonrefillable press dispensers. Bottles that can be opened can be tampered with, and I’ve found supposedly secured dispensers left unlocked. And housekeeping has to remember to fill them. I once returned to the same room at a Marriott Courtyard a week later and found the bath gel dispenser still empty. It had been empty throughout the previous stay, too. You usually discover that problem after you’ve gotten into the shower. Hotels have also given up something that helped sell both the stay and the toiletry brand. I started using Aesop after staying at the Park Hyatt Tokyo. Taking a little bottle home extended the experience and introduced me to a product I still use. The 42 Best Gifts for All Types of Travelers, From Luxe Flight Essentials to Road Trip Gadgets

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Shower your favorite five-star vagabonds with these treats for their next staycation or whirlwind adventure.

By Danielle Directo-Meston, Erin Lassner November 7, 2026 12:27pm

If you purchase an independently reviewed product or service through a link on our website, The Hollywood Reporter may receive an affiliate commission.

For some travelers, there’s nothing like rolling up to the gate with a luxe piece of luggage and well-prepared flight kit, or setting out on a road trip with all of the gear needed for a smooth ride. Some of the best gifts for travelers who love a luxurious experience don’t need to cost an arm and a leg — plenty of essentials can turn any trip into a five-star one.

For instance, compact toiletry bags with the perfect number compartments can keep them organized like a jet-setting pro, while comfy airport shoes make security checks feel like a breeze. Glampers will love a chic camera for capturing their memories. And for those bitten by the travel bug (and itching for their next adventure), a subscription to coffee or snacks from around the world will transport them back to their favorite places. Related Stories Lifestyle Parke's Collab With Stoney Clover Lane Is Back by (Very) Popular Demand Lifestyle Streaming More, Spending Less: T-Mobile's 5G Home Internet Deal Starts at $30 a Month

Check out our top picks below, from softside luggage by Hollywood-loved brand Away (Meghan Markle and Jessica Alba have been spotted with its gear) to red-eye-ready essentials from Tumi (F1 superstar Lando Norris and singer Reneé Rapp have starred in its campaigns) and more. Whether you’re shopping for holiday gifts, or you’re looking for presents for the frequent flyers and road-trippers in your life, we’ve rounded some of the best travel gifts for every type of adventurer.

Got a homesick transplant or someone who just wants to rep their home state? Celebrity-loved designer Maya Brenner’s pendant necklaces are available in a range of countries (including France, England, Italy, China, Japan, Mexico, Germany and more) and all 50 U.S. states. Each charm comes on a 16-inch cable chain in sterling silver or 14-karat gold. Meghan Markle, Abigail Spencer, Rihanna and Yvonne Strahovski and are just a few stars who’ve worn her pieces.

For only $130, Quince’s roomy polycarbonate shell carry-on has 360-degree spinner wheels, a sturdy aluminum telescopic trolley handle with soft rubber for comfort, interior compression panels, TSA-approved locks and a removable laundry bag. The capacity offers enough space for six to nine outfits, and there’s a 18-by-12.5-inch interior pocket for storage. Available in a range of colorways — both core and seasonal — Quince’s signature suitcase is also offered in a smaller carry-on size and multiple checked-in varieties.

Hoka’s Clifton 11 is the latest iteration of the brand’s versatile Clifton line. These everyday sneakers are one of our favorite shoes for traveling, thanks to their heel tab that makes pulling them on a breeze after TSA checks. The sturdy silhouette also makes it easy to run through airports and cities alike.

The Empire Carryall Bag 48 is genuinely our dream weekender, especially in the newer Loved Leather finish which only gets better with age. Bonus: Its intentional lived-in look means you don’t have to be too precious with it — this stunner is suited for even the craziest of travel days.

For a long day of travel, there’s no better companion than an insanely quenching mask, and 111Skin’s Celestial Black Diamond Eye Mask is just that. Specifically created to target tired-looking eyes, the cult-favorite skin product is a Hollywood magnet, with the brand’s famous fans including Anne Hathaway, Kim Kardashian, Jennifer Anniston and Bella Hadid.

At first glance, this duo looks like an ultra chic toiletry bag set. And yes, it is. But it’s also so much more. Lined with Pristinium, OTM’s signature temperature-protective material, each case is not just created to carry your products, but built to protect them — ensuring all your formulas work just as intended.

Perfect as a stocking stuffer gift for travelers who don’t want to leave their beauty and grooming routines at home, this 11-piece travel set comes with reusable bottles and containers in stylish black and white prints. It includes three flat squeeze pouches, a mini spray bottle, two pump bottles, two mini jars with resealable lids and a storage case, plus a funnel, spatula and pipette for easily refilling. Everything is TSA approved and BPA-free, too.

For the traveler who loves to keep their baggage ultra-light, Samsonite’s Proxis global carry-on spinner weighs just 4.7 pounds. The expandable suitcase is made of the company’s durable new Roxkin outer shell that’s designed to bounce back, while the built-in USB port lets jet-setters keep their tech powered. Other features include TSA-friendly locks, rubber spinner wheels and interior organizers and zippered pockets.

If there’s one thing that many travelers learned in the past few years, it’s that lost luggage can put a tailspin in your plans. A smart tracker — like Apple’s AirTag — can help travelers keep tabs on their belongings.

Maison Wheat’s super-soft basics are our go-to uniforms for flights and long car rides, thanks to their eco-friendly blend of Tencel modal and spandex that make for easy layering on the plane. Our favorite pairing? We love the women’s Knoxville ribbed tank with an elevated silhouette and the Los Angeles joggers with a comfy, elastic-free waistband and tapered look. For jet-setting men, we recommend the Oxford quarter zip or the San Diego sweatpant. (The brand was co-founded by actor Huw Collins and his wife, Molly Shaheen.)

When they can’t decamp to far-flung destinations or don’t want to leave their favorite fragrances at home, this trio of luxe perfumes by cult-cool luxury brand Byredo will keep recipients smelling chic during their travels. (Anne Hathaway, Rosie Huntington-Whiteley and stylist Courtney Mays have said they’re fans.)

Minimalist nature lovers won’t mind forgoing the fuss that comes with massive vacation homes with gift card for Postcard Cabins. Scarlett Johansson Is Responsible For Bobby Flay’s Meet-Cute (And I Did Not See This Coming)

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Justin Baldoni Cuts More Ties After Leaving LA Following Blake Lively Lawsuits

Insiders Claimed JLo Was Getting Too Serious About Brett Goldstein Too Quickly, But Not So Fast

Thank Goodness A Nobody Wants This Star And I Have The Same Weird Emotions About Morgan And Sasha

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Bobby Flay definitely hasn't left The Food Network, as he’s got plenty of competition shows to host amid the 2026 TV schedule. But, outside of cooking up a storm, he has also cooked up some sweet love with American chef and restaurateur Brooke Williamson. However, he didn’t do it alone. Flay recently credited actress Scarlett Johansson with his meet-cute romance, and I definitely wasn't expecting to get these details.

While Flay and Williamson interviewed with People, he spoke about hanging out with a friend who helped set him up with his romantic partner. Without name-dropping, he gave some hints like this friend being a huge movie star and being married to SNL’s Colin Jost. Of course, that latter detail was a dead giveaway regarding Johansson!

Flay apparently first met Williamson when they were both judges of Supermarket Stakeout back in 2020. However, he didn’t get together with the Top Chef winner until season 6 of BBQ Brawl five years later. Here’s his true tale of why it took him so long to ask Williamson out and how Johansson helped pick up the pace:

And I was just telling [Johansson] this quandary I was having that I was, like, totally falling for somebody that I work with. And I’m just frustrated because I’m not gonna go there. And I just don’t know what to do. And she told me I was out of my mind. That’s how I sort of came to terms with it. And literally on the very last day of that season of BBQ Brawl, I went up to [Williamson] and I was like, ‘I need to talk to you about something.’ And she’s like, ‘What?’ And I was like, ‘If you’re still interested…’ It had gone on for a while. ‘If you’re still interested, I’m ready to make a commitment to you.’ And that was sort of the beginning of the beginning. There was nothing but support and thrilled that we had decided to make it an actual thing.

Aww, that is too cute for words! It’s adorable that, for a man who knows how to put two and two together when cooking up a delicious dish, he had trouble putting himself together with his beautiful co-worker. I also can't help but love Johansson’s honesty in telling her friend to make a move. The actress may have only played Black Widow in the Marvel movies, but she was a real hero for Flay. And now, Flay and Williamson have been together for over a year.

Before Brooke Williamson, Flay was married three times, so he's certainly had his share of experience with relationships. With that in mind, Flay went on to explain to People what he wanted in a new romance and how that all aligned with Williamson:

I mean, to me, the most important thing is I want a life partner that I’m committed to. I don’t need all of the documentation. But at the same time, I need to make sure that Brooke feels like she’s committed to. We were in London and we were sitting on a bench in The Regent’s Park, and I was like, ‘I got you a gift.’ And I said, ‘But before I give you the gift, like, you’re cool with not getting married, right? Like, we’re on the same page?’ Because we had obviously talked about that. And she’s like, ‘Yeah, yeah, absolutely.’ I was like, ‘Okay.’

So Flay surprised his partner with a huge diamond ring that cemented his commitment to her without a wedding of sorts. To some, it may seem confusing to give the woman you love a ring without the promise of marriage. However, I find it pretty cute, as it’s like a promise ring that still pledges loyalty. And, of course, at the end of the day, it's Flay and Williamson's business as to what they want their relationship to be.

On that note, Bobby Flay and Brooke Williamson seem to be quite happy, and I love to see it. Also, kudos to Scarlett Johansson for intervening a playing a bit of cupid. Fans can watch Flay and Williamson’s Food Network shows using a Hulu subscription or an HBO Max subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

5 Of The Biggest Gas Discounts Available Through Reward Programs Right Now

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Since Trump's war with Iran kicked off in February of this year, gas prices have been on everyone's mind. Who could've predicted we'd see gas prices skyrocket as the result of such a war? Well, we did. Worry not, though, dear reader. Gas prices are sure to come crashing down any moment, especially now that three tankers have hit mines in the Strait of Hormuz. Maybe after such a flawless victory, we should start calling it the Strait of America.

In the face of gas averaging around $4.20 per gallon nationwide (as of this writing), and with no signs those numbers will drop significantly anytime soon, many of our readers have started budgeting to account for high fuel prices. Meanwhile, others are scouring their cities to find the best possible price. Right now, memberships and rewards programs are hands down the best ways to get a substantial discount at the fuel pump, though some good deals are unfortunately drying up, forcing buyers to pivot to new methods to secure the best price in town.

The most notable loss in recent weeks has been the death of the Kroger gas discount. Fortunately, Costco, Love's, Walmart, and other gas and grocery chains still have worthwhile incentives that can save you a ton at the gas station. If you're filling up a big tank or driving a lot, such deals stand to save you a lot of money for the foreseeable future. Stack these savings with a credit card with good rewards for gas purchases, and you might even be able to convince yourself it's 2024 again and gas prices are hovering just above $3 per gallon.

The first rewards program gas discount on the list is only temporary, but it's a big one. Regular Save Mart shoppers are going to want to strike while the iron is hot because this West Coast grocer is offering up to $1 off per gallon at participating Shell locations, but only through the end of 2026. Signing up to be a Save Mart Rewards member is free, and a dollar spent in store equals one point. You can turn 100 points into 10 cents off per gallon, and this benefit stacks up to 1,000 points for a total of $1 per gallon off. You can use these points at the pump by providing your Save Mart-associated phone number at a participating station.

If you live in Save Mart's service area, you're probably staring down gas prices of around $5.50 per gallon, so this gas deal represents up to 18% off at the pump. Not bad at all, though of course it requires spending about $1,000 on qualifying groceries to unlock the full discount. This deal was initially scheduled to end in September of 2026, and was extended, so there's a chance you'll be able to find it next year, too, but don't count on the supermarket's biggest fuel deal ever lasting that much longer.

There's some fine print you'll want to know about, too. First, you're limited to 20 gallons per fill-up, for a maximum savings of $20. Second, Save Mart points don't last forever. They expire around three months after they're earned, so you're heavily incentivized to use them regularly rather than accumulating points and waiting for the best deals to drop each year.

Perpetual fan-favorite wholesaler Costco promises deals of all types all the time. Those $1.50 hot dogs are famous for a reason, and they really are a helluva deal, but Costco fans swear by their gas prices. Full disclosure: I'm one of those people. Their fuel deals aren't part of a rewards program in the traditional sense. There's no spending to accumulate points and then spending points for further deals. Instead, access to Costco gas stations is locked behind a $65 per year membership that includes online and in-person wholesale shopping, membership for two people, and a long list of benefits from prescriptions and eye exams to car maintenance and insurance offerings. A Costco membership also gets you discounts on Volkswagen SUVs, if you're in the market for a new ride.

The elephant in the room with getting your gas from Costco is the frankly unbelievable lines you might see there. I actually refused to go for a long time, as the lines looked like too much of a headache to be worth the savings. However, Costco pushes fuel to a high volume of drivers surprisingly efficiently, thanks to their numerous pumps and clear signage that indicate when a new pump has opened up and who gets to use it. I've rarely had to wait more than a couple of minutes, and for 10 to 20 cents of savings per gallon, the wait is well worth it.

If you're trying to eke out as much value as possible, you can also sign up for the brand's credit card for 5% back on gas bought at Costco. Those who refill weekly can recover their membership fee with ease.

Love's Travel Stops have a lovable history, as well as a respectable business model, and of all the things they do right, their gas reward program might just be the cherry on top. One aspect of the program that sets it apart from much of the competition is that the Love's Rewards program takes a two-tiered approach, offering different incentives for "casual" drivers and those who take to the road as a profession.

For non-pros, this rewards program offers one point per gallon of fuel purchases, two points per dollar spent in the store, and an automatic 10 cents off every gallon of gasoline. Unlike the Save Mart program, points don't expire as long as your account remains in good standing and stays active. Additionally, members get up to 25 cents off auto diesel purchases. The Love's app also gets you access to basic Wi-Fi at travel stops as well as mobile deals and the ability to order ahead at their connected restaurants.

Professional drivers get assigned a membership tier based on how much fuel they've purchased in any given month. It starts at Base for up to 499 gallons purchased, Gold is unlocked at 500 gallons, and the Platinum and Diamond tiers start at 1,000 and 1,500 gallons, respectively. Members get benefits like free showers and drink refills depending on their tier and earn scaling points for gallons fueled, dollars spent in-store, and dollars spent on truck care. These incentives make OTR trucking just a little bit easier and a little bit more comfortable, which can go a long way to ensuring drivers stay safe and happy on the road.

The Albertsons forU program works like most other grocery store rewards programs, but it has one of the most impressive fuel rewards deals out there. Like other such programs, it unlocks personalized deals, digital coupons, and the occasional free item, and it can also recommend meal plans that include recipes you can add the entirety of to your cart with the click of a button. You get one point per dollar spent on groceries, though these points expire at the end of the month after they're acquired, which is a faster turnaround than even the Save Mart program. If you're the type of person who likes to give gift cards for the holidays, you can earn points faster, though, with a rate of two points per dollar. There are also periodic multiplier events to help you rack up points.

The gas perks apply to gas stations across the country, and the membership generally saves you 10 cents per gallon at Walmart fueling stations, as well as CITGO, Mobil, Murphy, and Exxon stations. For those with Sam's Club memberships, though, the discount doesn't stack. Instead, Walmart+ simply gets you access to Sam's Club stations at Sam's Club member prices. If you find yourself in Alabama, you may also notice the discount isn't consistent, landing somewhere between five and 10 cents depending on the location.

Getting the gas perk requires keeping the Walmart app on your phone, and you'll either need to scan a QR code at the pump or use a discount ID provided by the app. It's not as convenient as other options, but it's still a solid discount. This option doesn't earn your membership money back quite as fast as the Costco offer does, though, so it's probably reserved for people who can take as much advantage of the other perks as possible.

United Airlines Pilots Defy ATC Orders To Fly Through “The Worst Part” Of A Severe Thunderstorm

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Journalist – Aaron is an aviation and travel journalist with 14 years of experience in the travel and tourism industry. His background spans airport operations, aviation infrastructure, and travel consulting, having worked with global organizations across New Zealand, Europe, and North America. Combining industry expertise with firsthand travel experience across more than 52 countries and five continents, Aaron covers airlines, airports, aviation policy, and the wider travel sector. He is based in Canterbury, New Zealand. His work has been published in Simple Flying and featured by MSN, SAM CHUI Aviation & Travel, EIN News, Pakistan Defence, and Climate Depot.

United Airlines pilots scheduled to operate a nonstop flight from San Antonio International Airport (SAT) to Washington Dulles International Airport (IAD) refused to accept the air traffic control's proposed routing to the US capital, after they identified that the route would take them directly through a severe thunderstorm.

A recording shared by Aviation MP4 shows the moment the pilot questioned the clearance from ATC, which, instead of taking the service around the worst part of the weather, put the jet on a trajectory to travel through the worst part of heavy rain and thunderstorms. The pilot in command ultimately has the final say as to whether the flight can operate, as they hold the operational responsibility and safety for the flight. For this event, the pilots' concern was also backed up by United dispatch.

United was scheduled to operate flight 471 from San Antonio to Washington Dulles on Friday, November 6. As the plane had pushed back from the gate and was taxiing to the runway, the recording caught the moment that the crew queried the proposed route. Initially, air traffic control had rerouted the Boeing 737-700 to depart south and travel around the severe weather.

During the flight's taxi, the moments shared had the flight crew challenging the decision, with the pilot noting that he was going to speak with United dispatch, and that the crew was refusing to fly through what would have been up to four hours of challenging weather conditions for the crew, and unfavorable comfort for the passengers on board.

Simple Flying reached out to United for comment, and the airline confirmed the flight landed safely. It is believed that this incident occurred on Friday, November 6, but has not been confirmed by Simple Flying

Pilots onboard the flight quickly noted that they were unable to fly the proposed route, instead requesting that the initial routing, which would have taken the Washington-bound flight south of the severe weather. The air traffic control reevaluated the plan, while the United pilots spoke to airline dispatch, which backed them up, noting that they should not be flying through the adverse weather.

In the end, the crew was permitted to fly an alternative route, highlighting the authority that pilots have to reject an unsafe clearance request and opt for an alternative. While air traffic control provides the instructions and proposed flight paths, pilots in command have the final responsibility for the aircraft's safety, and all of those onboard, including passengers and crew.

United flight 471 is a daily service from San Antonio to Washington Dulles that has a scheduled departure time of 7:00 AM, with an expected arrival time into Washington of 11:13 AM. The usual flight duration is two hours and 45 minutes. For the proposed route through the thunderstorm would have added almost an hour of extra flying time according to the pilot's commentary in the video shared above.

The lasting impacts of the unthinkable on ATC.

The Pilot-In-Command (PIC) holds the final authority over the operation and safety of an aircraft. They are able to challenge or disagree with air traffic control or airline dispatch if they believe that the situation could be unsafe. Pilots have specific rights and protocols that they are allowed to use.

This can include rejecting clearance and requesting an alternative. They can also declare an emergency and deviate from any air traffic control clearance without prior permission if safety is compromised.

From San Antonio, United and United Express maintain regular scheduled flights to Chicago O'Hare International Airport (ORD), Denver (DEN), Houston George Bush Intercontinental Airport (IAH), Newark (EWR), San Francisco International Airport (SFO), and Washington Dulles.

Steam Bails Out Dead by Daylight After Review Bombing Campaign

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Dead by Daylight's recent Steam rating is back to Mostly Positive, but it wasn't a sudden change of heart among players that got it there. Valve has excluded a wave of reviews posted during the fallout over Behaviour Interactive's dealings with streamer TheBurntPeanut.

Chunky joins the ever-growing list of horror icons removed from Dead by Daylight, leading to fans questioning how well Behaviour can manage licenses

Reviews submitted between November 1 and November 6 have been marked as off-topic after the game was hit by a review-bombing campaign. As Dexerto reports, the decision reversed a slide to Mixed, leaving an asterisk beside the game's Steam score.

Visit the Dead by Daylight Steam page and you will see a notice explaining that reviews from an unusual period of activity have been excluded from the displayed score. At the time of writing, 74% of its recent reviews are positive, putting the game back in the Mostly Positive category.

That doesn't mean Valve has deleted thousands of angry reviews. The reviews are still there; they simply don't count toward the score most Steam users see.

This is part of a system Valve introduced in 2019 to deal with review bombs centered on matters it considers unrelated to the experience of playing a game. It identifies a period of unusually heavy review activity, investigates it, and can exclude that period from the score shown to most shoppers by default.

You can still change your Steam Review Score Settings to include those reviews if you want the unfiltered picture. The filter covers the entire date range, not just the negative posts about a particular controversy. Even an ordinary gameplay review written between November 1 and November 6 is left out of the default calculation.

The reviews are still there; they simply don't count toward the score most Steam users see.

The review bomb was also coming from two directions. Some players objected to Behaviour associating itself with TheBurntPeanut, while others were angered by the developer's decision to distance itself from him. Both groups ended up expressing their frustrations on Steam, even though neither grievance was primarily about how Dead by Daylight plays.

The trouble began on October 30, when TheBurntPeanut played Dead by Daylight on stream and a member of Behaviour's team granted him access to the game's paid DLC, including characters and perks. The studio later explained that staff had spotted an opportunity to introduce the game to a large new audience, but the gift went ahead without the usual internal approvals.

Players questioned Behaviour's willingness to support a creator whose previous content they felt conflicted with the inclusive environment the developer says it wants to maintain.

Things only got worse when TheBurntPeanut offered to return the DLC and an official Dead by Daylight account responded with, "WE CAN TAKE THE HEAT! THEY ARE YOURS TO KEEP." The reply made it appear that the team was dismissing concerns before it had even considered them.

Behaviour issued an initial apology on November 1. In a longer statement on November 3, it acknowledged that staff had moved too quickly, admitted that its response had escalated the dispute, and confirmed that the gifted content had been removed from the streamer's account.

The developer also promised to rewrite its creator guidelines, tighten the process for approving gifts, and provide further training to its staff. It said it was taking internal action over the incident, although it wouldn't disclose details about individual employees.

The apology hasn't pleased everyone. Some players who objected to the DLC gift still question Behaviour's judgment, while some of TheBurntPeanut's supporters believe the studio caved to pressure. The divide is especially noticeable in a game that, as director Mathieu Côté explained in a June interview with DualShockers, has several distinct communities.

Valve's filter has improved the rating prospective buyers see, but it hasn't done anything to settle the dispute behind the reviews.

GTA 6 PS5 Physical Boxes Have Hit The Wild, A Harbinger Of Our Disc-Less Future

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6Grand Theft Auto VIGTA 6Physical GamesPS5Rockstar Games

We’re closing in on Grand Theft Auto 6‘s launch, which includes store shelves stocked with empty boxes. Physical cases for the PlayStation 5 version of the game are already appearing in the wild ahead of stores being allowed to sell them on November 12.

Photos of a shrink-wrapped copy of GTA 6 for PS5 appeared on the GTA6unmoderrated subreddit earlier today and were apparently posted by a German retail worker. It looked exactly how you’d expect it to, with a white label around a translucent blue plastic case. A warning label on the front of the box art reminds consumers that there’s no actual disc inside the physical box, and that an internet connection is required in order to redeem the digital download using the code inside.

A look at the physical Grand Theft Auto VI box

Image: u/IseeIceTea pic.twitter.com/qT9OhuFVwa

— CharlieIntel (@charlieINTEL) November 7, 2026

The user has since deleted the thread and their Reddit account, potentially worried that the leak might be traced back to them given Take-Two’s track record and the high-alert status everyone will be on in the lead up to the game’s November 19 launch. Of course, not shipping the game on a physical disc is one way of cutting down on these exact kind of leaks that can arise from stores breaking street dates on warehouse workers potentially stealing early copies.

GTA 6 is available to being preloading on November 10, with physical copies being distributed starting November 12 for those who pre-ordered them. People will be able to take the box home a full week before the game is actually live on North American servers. Take-Two’s decision not to release GTA 6 on a disc gives us our first test run of the disc-less future Sony has promised starting in 2028.

Some publishers will likely also use codes in boxes to maintain a similar presence in brick-and-mortar stores. They’ll leave diehard fans with the option to go around collecting empty display boxes if they wish. I will certainly be one of them.
